This can happen when a component repeatedly calls setState inside componentWillUpdate or componentDidUpdate. React limits the number of  \r\n  nested updates to prevent infinite loops.                                                                                                                                      \r\n      at setRef (composeRefs.tsx:11:5)                                                                                                                                           \r\n      at composeRefs.tsx:22:45                                                                                                                                                   \r\n      at Array.forEach                                                                                               \r\n      ...                                                                                                                                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  The setRef function in @radix-ui/react-compose-refs recursively triggers itself, causing React to hit the maximum update depth limit.                                          \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  In React 19, ref callback behavior changed - ref callbacks can now return cleanup functions, and React may call ref callbacks differently than in React 18. When setRef calls  \r\n  ref(value) at line 11, it triggers a re-render which invokes setRef again via the composed ref callback at line 22, creating an infinite loop.    \r\n\r\n\r\n### Expected behavior\r\n\r\n  Composed refs should work without causing infinite update loops in React 19.                                                                                                    \r\n\r\n### Reproducible example\r\n\r\nI have platejs plugins with radix popovers and radix tooltips on them. If I write fast inside plate, the page breaks. \r\nI\'m on a Macbook M1 Pro with 32 GB. One way to trigger this error is to inspect chrome and start a performance recording and start typing real fast. On Safari, for example, it doesn\'t break. I tried on restarted browser, guest mode. I\'m not the only one\r\n\r\n[CodeSandbox Template](n/a)\r\n\r\n### Suggested solution\r\n\r\n  The composeRefs and useComposedRefs functions need to handle React 19\'s new ref callback behavior. Possible approaches:                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  1. Memoize the composed ref callback to prevent unnecessary re-invocations                                                                                                     \r\n  2. Add a guard to prevent recursive setRef calls                                                                                                                               \r\n  3. Handle the cleanup function return value from React 19 ref callbacks       \r\n\r\n### Additional context\r\nRight now, it works fine with react 18 (even on next.js 16+) and we had to downgrade.\r\n\r\nI also saw this reddit post https://www.reddit.com/r/react/comments/1nnvwsp/react_19_causes_maximum_update_depth_exceeded/ where a user raised a similar case\r\n\r\nReact 19 introduced changes to ref callback behavior, including support for cleanup functions returned from ref callbacks. This may be causing the ref to be re-invoked on each render cycle.                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  Related stack trace also shows Slate editor involvement:                                                                                                                       \r\n  at So `handleRef` gets called 2 additional times per keystroke → "should call function ref only once" fails (it gets called 1 + 2×3 = 7 times).\n\nAnd this ref re-attach per render is exactly the mechanism behind the infinite loop in the bug report: when the ref callback does setState (as Slate/Plate\'s ref callback does via onContextChange/onChange), each re-render re-attaches the ref, which calls setState again → infinite loop → "Maximum update depth exceeded."\n\nWhy didn\'t this happen in React 18? {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Now I have the full picture:\n\n**History:**\n1. `f64d7815` (#3477): Fixed the bug where the function ref was being re-invoked on every render. The fix: use `useComposedRefs` (a hook) inside `SlotClone`, and additionally added `\'use client\';` to index.ts. The test "given an Input" was added (both asChild and non-asChild variants, "should only call function refs once").\n2. `e59256f1` (#3554): Reverted. The reasons were "RSC errors" — the changes that were reverted included the removal of `\'use client\'` from index.ts. That means the RSC errors were actually caused by the `use client` directive + hooks in Slot? The revert removed the `\'use client\'` line from index.ts (i.e., #3477 had added `\'use client\'`), reverted to `composeRefs`, and skipped the test.\n\nSo the "underlying issue" is: we need a stable composed ref (the memoization fix), but we cannot use `useComposedRefs` (a hook) inside Slot. Reasons:\n- Slot must work in server components (RSC). Adding `use client` to the slot package changed its behavior (it made slot a client-only package, breaking usage from RSC/server components) — that\'s what caused the RSC errors that forced the revert.\n\nHmm, wait, let me actually double-check: #3477 added `\'use client\'` to index.ts (the revert removed it). Why did they add `use client`? Because `useComposedRefs` calls `React.useCallback` — a hook. For Slot to be usable in a client component, the hook is fine, but if Slot is imported into a server component... in RSC, components with hooks can\'t be rendered on the server unless the package is marked `use client` (which makes it a client reference from the server). So #3477 marked slot as `use client` to make the hook work in the RSC pipeline, but that broke users who were using Slot inside server components (since a `use client` package can only be referenced from the server, not rendered there... actually, a `use client` component can be rendered from a server component as a client reference — but things like `Slottable` that get evaluated on the server would break, and passing functions across the boundary would break too).\n\nSo the constraints for the correct fix:\n1. Make the composed ref identity stable across renders (fixes the infinite loop / re-invocation of refs).\n2. Do NOT add hooks to Slot (no `useComposedRefs`), and don\'t add `use client` — keep Slot usable in RSC/server components.\n3. Keep everything else working (nested slottable, lazy, Fragment, merging).\n\nHow do we memoize without hooks? A pure `composeRefs` creates a new function every render. Options:\n\n**Option A: Cache the composed ref in a module-level WeakMap keyed by the pair of refs?** Hacky and not thread-safe for RSC (module state shared across requests... a WeakMap keyed by function identity would leak across server requests — bad for RSC, and could even cause incorrect behavior if the same function instance is reused).\n\n**Option B: Make the composed ref a stable object per (forwardedRef, childRef) pair using a memoization function that doesn\'t use hooks and doesn\'t use module state.** Same module-state problem.\n\n**Option C: Change the approach — instead of creating a new composed function every render, pass both refs to React via a ref object? No — React\'s ref prop only takes one ref.**\n\n**Option D: Make `composeRefs` itself return a function with a stable identity...** You can\'t get stability across calls without state.\n\n**Option E: Avoid the re-attachment entirely another way — make Slot not re-render at all when only the child\'s value changes?** Can\'t control that. The parent re-renders Slot.\n\nHmm wait, actually, let me reconsider. What is it that actually triggers the loop? A re-render → new composed ref identity → React detaches (old(null)) + re-attaches (new(node)) → if the user\'s ref callback does setState/onChange → loop.\n\nAlternative fix: **memoize the composed callback with `React.useMemo`-style semantics using a WeakMap stored on the ref function itself?** That\'s a per-render stateless cache: attach properties to the function? We can\'t reliably modify the user\'s function.\n\n**Option F: Reconsider — is memoization even the right fix?** What does the test require? "should only call function refs once" — the ref must not be re-invoked on re-render. The only way to prevent React from re-attaching a callback ref is a stable identity. So yes, we need a stable identity for the composed ref.\n\n**Option G: Use a ref object (not a callback) as the merged ref.** We can create a single RefObject whose `.current` is composed: on every render, set `mergedRefObj.current = ...`? No — React only calls the callback ref when the identity changes. An object ref is stable by identity. We could do this:\n\n```tsx\nfunction useMergedRef(...) // hook — not allowed (RSC).\n```\n\nWithout hooks, we can\'t create per-component-instance state...\n\nWait. Hold on. Can Slot really not use hooks? The issue is RSC. In RSC, when a server component renders `<Slot>`, the function body of Slot runs on the server. Calling `React.useCallback` on the server in RSC... let me think: does React\'s server renderer (react-server) implement `useCallback`? RSC\'s supported hooks: `useState`, `useSyncExternalStore`, `useRef` are NOT supported (throw). What about `useCallback`? Let me recall react-server\'s hooks: the `react-server` condition exports a React with a subset: `useState` throws "Cannot read state of a component in Server Component"... actually, I recall that `useMemo` and `useCallback` are supported in RSC (they\'re on the allowlist — React uses them internally on the server too). Hmm, but the revert message says "RSC errors" — maybe the error was from `useComposedRefs` → `useCallback` throwing in RSC? Or was it from the `use client` directive?\n\nThe revert removed both: the hook usage AND `\'use client\'`. The changeset says "Revert slot changes causing errors in server components". The slot changes = probably the `use client` (making @radix-ui/react-slot a client-only package, which broke people who were importing Slot/Slottable in server components — e.g., the very common Radix pattern `<Popover asChild>` used in server components, or the Slot component itself getting evaluated on the server and throwing).\n\nSo the constraint is confirmed: the fix must be hook-free (at least not one that requires `use client`).\n\nNow — how did actual Radix fix this eventually? Let me think... I believe in the actual radix-ui/primitives repo, this is still open (the test is still skipped upstream). This task is essentially: implement what #3477 tried, but in a way that doesn\'t break RSC.\n\n**Hook-free per-instance memoization trick:** A per-render WeakMap is stateful across renders (module state) — bad for RSC (server module state is shared across requests, and per-instance caches keyed by ref identity could cross requests: if request A and request B use the same function instance... on the server, function instances are per-render anyway unless memoized. Still risky and leaks memory).\n\n**Better trick: exploit the fact that React itself compares refs by identity, and instead of memoizing the function, prevent the "re-attach" behavior another way — by making the composed callback\'s identity stable across renders when its inputs are stable, using a deterministic cache on the inputs.** A WeakMap with a compound key...\n\nHold on, let me reconsider. Maybe there\'s a cleaner approach that the React team has suggested for exactly this: **store the composed function on the element itself?** No.\n\n**Another clean approach:** In React 19, ref callbacks support cleanup. What if Slot passed a single stable callback whose identity never changes, and that callback dynamically reads the current child ref from a shared mutable cell? The "cell" needs per-instance state... hooks again.\n\nHmm, wait — is per-instance state really unavailable without hooks? What about `useRef`? That\'s also a hook. In RSC, does `useRef` throw? Let me check: does RSC support `useRef`? I think react-server doesn\'t support useRef (it throws "Cannot useRef..."). Hmm, actually I\'m not sure.\n\nLet me check what the react-server condition actually supports. From React\'s source (ReactSharedInternals in react-server): the hooks implemented in the server renderer include: `useState` (throws? no...). Actually, from my memory of the react-server exports: `ReactServer.useState` exists and returns an initial-value-only state (server components are rendered only once — state is static!). Yes! In RSC, `useState` is supported but updates throw? No...\n\nFrom React\'s docs on server components: "Hooks that are supported in Server Components: useState (read-only? no...)" — actually, the documented list: RSC supports `useState`? The docs say "In Server Components, you can use useState, but the state will never change"? I don\'t remember exactly.\n\nThe practical approach: verify empirically.
